The selected hike dayJ1 MAIN · J3 SHORTER · J7 POOR WEATHER
HIKEMAIN · SELECTED · NO BOOKING · Björkliden – Gohpasvággi (valley and stream) · 6.8 km return, 309 m, full route unconfirmed; AllTrails distance; 3–4 h. The official Björkliden page describes only the Gohpasjohka plateau, ~1.5 km from the hotel (~3 km return), which is the fixed turnaround · 121 km, 1 h 32; allow 1 h 50–2 h 05 · leave 07:30 · walk 09:35–09:50 · turn back by 11:30 · car 12:35–13:50 (14:15 at the latest) · ICEHOTEL 14:40–16:10 (worst case ~16:35). Whole day 7 h 10–8 h 40, of which ~3 h 40–4 h 10 is driving · Hotell Fjället, Björkliden
SHORTER · SELECTED · NO BOOKING · Kiruna hills, close to the hotel: Luossavaara "Midnattssolsstigen" · ~4.3 km one way, point to point: starts at Camp Ripan and "ends at the parking area at the base of the mountain". ~2 h (Ripan) or 1½–2½ h (Kiruna Lappland), easy to moderate. Snowshoes if there's snow. 360° view over Kiruna, and Kebnekaise when clear · ~25 min · Two-car shuttle: leave one car at the Luossavaara base car park (exact pin unconfirmed) and drive the other to Camp Ripan. Start by ~13:00; whole outing ~3½–4½ h (estimate) · Camp Ripan, Kiruna

Adam, 10 and 17 Sep: on every driving day from Sundsvall on, the paved route is the plan on the card and the TET line is an alternative, decided on the morning — first the three Swedish transfer days on S-07, S-07 + S-06 and S-16 and the saved S-06 line from the gate to Arvidsjaur on 1 Oct, gravel first (881 km of trail, measured from Sweden TET.gpx, 137,747 track points), then the eight Finnish ones. Kilometres from the 2026 Finland TET.gpx and Sweden TET.gpx, summed point to point — floors where the file is drawn coarsely; road links from Google Maps (fastest route, 10–11 Sep 2026; the 1 Oct roads from OSRM, 23–24 Sep). Hours at 35 km/h on recorded gravel or unrecorded track and 65 on recorded tarmac, + 1 h of stops, from first light — surfaces from Trafikverket NVDB and Digiroad, sampled about every kilometre on 22–23 Sep (the 8, 13, 15 and 17 Oct lines on 24 Sep) — up to ~6 km apart where the GPX is drawn coarsely, so since v1.30 each sample counts for the stretch around it: of the 2,232 km of line, ~640 km reads paved, ~1,060 km gravel, ~532 km with no record. The road plans' hours are driving time with no stop allowance (the road-leg convention); add an hour before comparing. all twelve taken2,232 kmSweden 881 km (the 1 Oct line's 224 among them), Finland 1,351 km (the 8, 11 and 15 Oct lines' 123, 191 and 197 among them) — ~640 km recorded paved, ~1,060 km recorded gravel, ~532 km with no record; Koli → Rantasalmi on 21 Oct has no line worth the detour; the ICEHOTEL → Levi Pallas piece and the Levi loop are on the base cards; the Ivalo, Rovaniemi and Ruka lines are the 8, 11 and 15 Oct base days
Fuel on the Swedish lines, in order: Timrå · Viksjö · Mjällom · Ullånger · Skorped · Trehörningsjö · Norrfors · (Rödåsel, on the Vindeln extension only) · Lycksele and Arvidsjaur on the road · Moskosel · Vidsel · Nattavaara (8 km off) · Hakkas. Longest gaps on the line: Viksjö → Mjällom 109 km on 28 Sep, Norrfors → the Granö turn-off 75 km on the 29th. Nothing exceeds a tank; fill at Ullånger (before the hotel road to Docksta), Granö or Lycksele, and Arvidsjaur regardless.
TURN-BACK RULE, on any trail day taken. If by midday the trail is running slow — surface worse than expected, a section closed, a vehicle in trouble — drop to the road for the rest of the day: the E4 shadows S-07 and the southern S-06 from Härnösand to Umeå, road 363 and the E12 from Granö, road 97 from Harads. On 2 October do not hesitate: two paid nights at ICEHOTEL are waiting, and the road via Gällivare is the surest way in.

On the sealed legs, light is not the constraint — distance is; even the shortest day gives close to 11 h between first and last light. On the trail days nothing is assumed about pace, which is why they start at first light and carry a turn-back rule.

Verified · Evidence and limits

The S-06 line, 1 Oct — the gravel day (selected: the saved S-06 route, S-06 km 452 → 228 — 224.1 km by the file, 224 in the roadbook (225 before v 1.30) — driven reversed, gravel first; estimates: routing, the GPX and Trafikverket road register, 22–24 Sep). 11 km of road to the gate at km 228 (~6 km gravel), then the line south-west: Vidsel (km 281, fuel), Storforsen (km 294, on the line — ~30 min at the rapids inside the hour of stops, the only visit; the Döda fallet walk, 1.4 km, is not in it; the old "5 km off at km 260" was wrong), Moskosel (km 372, fuel), Arvidsjaur (km 452, lunch, fuel) — then 175 km home by 94, Älvsbyn, 356, BD 683 and 97 (road register 166 samples, 155 paved, 11 gravel). 224 km of trail (~15 tarmac · ~208 gravel · ~1 no record), 410 km in all, ~225 km of it recorded gravel; driving ~9.3 h + 1 h of stops, 06:30 → ~16:50, last light 18:56. Exits (routed and sampled 24 Sep): Moskosel later than ~11:45 → home by E45 · 374 · BD 668 · BD 682 · 97, 127 km, ~45 gravel, ~2.6 h — or E45 to Arvidsjaur (55 km, ~4 gravel) and the tarmac home, ~3.7 h; Vidsel → home 54 km, ~42 gravel, ~1.4 h. The shorter way home from Arvidsjaur, BD 660 · 668 · 682, is 141 km with ~47 km of gravel — the same ~2.8 h. Corrected 24 Sep: v 1.24's late exit, "Moskosel → E45 · 374 · Älvsbyn home on tarmac, 206 km", had never been surface-sampled. Set aside on 23 Sep: the S-06 + S-16 loop of v 1.18 (220 km, ~155 gravel, 7.4 h) and the other sampled shapes considered previously. Storforsen (Länsstyrelsen Norrbotten, 23 Sep): a large car park, ploughed in winter; toilets, a shelter and fireplaces listed; "däckspång leder ut mot forsen" — the boardwalk to the rapids; "branta stup … klipphällarna kan vara hala när de är våta" — cliffs, slippery rock when wet; no fishing, fires only at the fireplaces; a fee is not mentioned. Hotell Storforsen (storforsen.se, 23 Sep): lunch buffet every day 12–14, SEK 152 — the line now passes at ~08:35, before it opens; à la carte Mon–Sat 18–21:30, Sun 17–19:30, booking required. lansstyrelsen.se storforsen.se

Bonnstan, Skellefteå's church town, 30 Sep — optional on the way (optional; Gammelstad stays the main stop). A short loop off the E4 through Skellefteå, +3.5 km on the day (347.1 km with it, 343.6 without), all tarmac (routing and road register, 24 Sep); leave Granö 09:30 → Bonnstan ~11:45 → ~30 min → Gammelstad ~14:30 → Treehotel ~16:35; skipped, Gammelstad ~13:55 and Treehotel ~16:05. bonnstan.se (24 Sep): 114 houses and 376 chambers today — a fire in 2012 destroyed two houses with sixteen chambers; privately owned — "Kamrarna i Skellefteå är privatägda" — a listed building ("byggnadsminne") since 1982; the present houses date from the mid-1830 s, after a fire; the car park is at 64.75199, 20.93015. Urho Kekkonen National Park from Saariselkä / Kiilopää, ~30–40 km south — trails usable until first snow, usually mid-October; day huts open. Kaunispää 2.1 km, ~1 h · Luulampi 11 km, 4–5 h, day hut · Kiirunapolku 6 km from Kiilopää, graded moderate to hard. Trail cafés closed — carry food (7 Sep). luontoon.fi Rumakuru/I3 is omitted following the corrected menu; use I1/I2/I4/I5. Thu 8 Oct · SELECTED · NO BOOKING · observed Thu 24 Sep; current availability unconfirmed — More

SELECTED · NO BOOKING · Thu 8 Oct · as of Thu 24 Sep; current availability unconfirmed.

depart 08:00 · first light 06:51 on the line ~08:40 · gravel ~09:05 Angeli ~10:05 · the way back Karigasniemi ~11:45 · fuel Aurora Village ~14:55 with the hour of stops last light 19:01 · 4.1 h in hand

Action: Follow the route, cut rules and weather choice; no booking action. No deadline published.

Verified · Evidence and limits

The FIN-07 line north of Inari, Thu 8 Oct — the gravel day (selected, in place of the I1 loop; estimates — routing, the GPX and road register, 24 Sep). 41 km of road 4 to Inari; the line from the Siida door (km 667) west on Angelintie (road 9553; the first ~26 km tarmac, ~3 km of it no record) to Angeli (km 728) and north along the Inarijoki — the Norwegian border river — to Karigasniemi (km 790); home by roads 92 and 4, 139 km (tarmac, ~2 km no record). 123 km of trail (~35 tarmac · ~81 gravel · ~7 no record), 304 km in all; driving ~5.8 h + 1 h of stops, 08:00 → ~14:55, last light 19:01; an hour at Siida on the way home (last tickets 16:15) → ~15:55. Way off: at Angeli later than ~13:00 → back the way you came, 103 km (~33 gravel), ~2.0 h. Road condition: drivers' forum reports 2006–2021 disagree, washboard to fairly good — look at the road 9553 camera ↗ the night before; gates mapped in OSM within ~60 m of the line near km 686, 757, 767, 783 and 786 — on the road or not, open or locked: unknown. Fuel Inari, Karigasniemi. Not the border zone — that is Inari's Russian side. Replaced: I1, FIN-01 east of the E75 south of Vuotso and back — 88 km of trail after ~105 km of road, 6.6 h.

Verified · Evidence and limits

The FIN-07 line, Sun 11 Oct — the gravel day (selected: the line that was the 10 Oct trail alternative, driven whole as a day from the base, gravel first; estimates — routing, the GPX and road register, 22–24 Sep). 4 km of road 81 to FIN-07 km 277, then the line north with its numbering: Ounasvaara (km ~280 — the first ~13 km read tarmac; no record from about km 290, recorded gravel from about km 302), east of Meltaus (km 344), Kierinki (km 409, fuel), to km 468 on road 80 — then 179 km of tarmac home by roads 80, 79 and 934, 161 of 161 samples paved. 191 km of trail (~38 tarmac · ~50 gravel · ~103 no record), 374 km in all — ~50 km of it recorded gravel, ~103 km with no record; driving ~7.8 h + 1 h of stops, 07:30 → ~16:20, last light 18:58. Exits: at Kierinki later than 12:30 (plan ~11:00) → home from there, 116 km by Kieringintie, 952 and 934 — ~88 km paved, ~27 km gravel, ~2.1 h of driving from Kierinki (leave at 12:30 → ~14:40); Meltaus (km 344) → home 69 km, ~12 km gravel or unrecorded. Corrected 24 Sep: v 1.23 gave the Kierinki exit as "road 79, 115 km" — it is roads 952 and 934 with gravel. The day replaces R1 (the Kierinki half, 133 km of line); the ATV moves to Monday 13:00. Use the distances and cut rules above; the older escape instruction is superseded.

Recorded detail — Sodankylä, Sat 10 Oct — the stopover on the drive down (selected: "find a stopover"; on road 4 — +0.4 km by routing, all tarmac in road register, 24 Sep) · current availability unconfirmed — More

Sodankylä, Sat 10 Oct — the stopover on the drive down (selected: "find a stopover"; on road 4 — +0.4 km by routing, all tarmac in road register, 24 Sep). Andreas Alariesto museum-gallery, Jäämerentie 3 — 10 € (8 € pensioners, students and schoolchildren; free with the Museum Card), guide included; Alariesto's paintings of Lapland life, over 100 works. Hours: the gallery's own page gives Tue–Sat 11–17 and, dated 1 Oct 2026 – 31 May 2027, Tue–Sat 10–16 — it contradicts itself; the 12:35 visit fits either (alariestomuseo.fi, read 24 Sep; +358 40 158 7970 to be sure). The old church (1689) beside it — Finland's best-preserved 17 th-century wooden church by the parish's own account; its page reads "VANHA KIRKKO SULJETTU" (the old church closed), so the outside and the churchyard only. Leave Ivalo 10:00 → Sodankylä ~12:35 → an hour → Glass Resort ~15:25. alariestomuseo.fi museot.fi sodankylanseurakunta.fi

Verified · Evidence and limits

Recorded detail — Lampivaara amethyst mine, Luosto — the longer option on 10 Oct, instead of Sodankylä: +29 km (315 km in all against the road plan's 286, routing; tarmac in road… · current availability unconfirmed — More

Lampivaara amethyst mine, Luosto — the longer option on 10 Oct, instead of Sodankylä: +29 km (315 km in all against the road plan's 286, routing; tarmac in road register, 24 Sep). October: Tue–Sat 11:00–15:00, guided mine tours from Lampivaara café on the hour, the last at 15:00; adult 27 €; tickets at the café, "no need to book tickets in advance"; each visitor digs a stone to keep. The mine is on the top of Lampivaara in Pyhä-Luosto national park and private cars may not drive up: 2.5 km uphill on a wide ski track from the Ukko-Luosto car park (Lampivaarantie 3) ⌖ PARK, "about 45 minutes", then ~150 steps up and 63 down to the pit (amethystmine.fi, read 24 Sep). Leave Ivalo 09:00 → the car park ~12:10 → ~3 h (our allowance: the walk up, the 13:00 tour, the walk down; the tour's length is not stated) → Glass Resort ~16:55. amethystmine.fi

D2 · Skuleskogen, Entré Väst: Nylandsruten viewing deck + Långtjärnhällorna loop

Lentiira, 17 Oct — the lakeside smoke sauna, 45–50 € an hour for two plus 15 € per extra person (~75–80 € for four); arrange the heating time when booking dinner (recorded earlier, 23 Sep). Bear Centre (Wild Brown Bear Ltd, Kostamustie 5644, Vartius) — the hides next door, 29 of them, wolverines and a wolf pack as well as bears. Its current products all end 10 Oct: "Bear Photography in Autumn" 16 Aug – 10 Oct from 199 € (briefing 15:00–16:00, hides until 08:00 next morning), "Bear Watching – Evening Trip" 1 Apr – 10 Oct from 99 € (15:30–~20:00), night trip 159 €; the old wildbrownbear.fi page still says "open daily 1 st April – 30 th October" and 140 € a night, but it is © 2018 and superseded by bearcentre.fi. Nothing for 17 Oct unless they say otherwise — +358 40 5469008; an overnight hide would in any case clash with the 07:10 departure on the 18 th. Boreal Wildlife Centre, Viiksimo, 60 km from Kuhmo — "September and October offer great opportunities" for wolf, bear and wolverine; one night 220 € pp; request form. bearcentre.fi

Recorded map reference · Recorded map reference · Recorded map reference

Verified · Evidence and limits

Recorded detail — Reindeer on the roads are a year-round hazard on every unfenced road, worst at dusk — slow right down, they move in groups, and a collision must be reported · current availability unconfirmed — More

Reindeer on the roads are a year-round hazard on every unfenced road, worst at dusk — slow right down, they move in groups, and a collision must be reported. Seeing them on the trail is a separate matter: unlikely in September, the herds are still high.

Finnish local time is one hour ahead of Sweden during the trip; at the border move watches one hour forward. Carry a headlamp per person; aim to be off trails an hour before last light.

WeatherTHE NEXT 15 DAYS · THE STATION RECORDS · KIT

Forecast at each base · as far as the models see, about 15 daysLoads when the page has a connection
DateBaseHigh / low °CPrecip mmSnow cmWind · gusts m/sSkyOfficial page
No forecast on this device yet — open the page once with a connection.

A planning overview, not route weather: one Open-Meteo request (its best-match model) for the fourteen villages, made when the page is opened or brought back with a connection and the saved answer is more than an hour old, or when the stamp above is tapped. The answer is kept on this device, so with no signal the page shows the last forecast it saw, with its age; older than a day it is dimmed. High / low are the warmest and coldest hours of that calendar day at the village point (the low usually falls before dawn); precip is the day's total as water, snow separately as fallen depth; wind is the day's strongest, gusts beside it. The point is the village or resort centre — Ruka, Levi and Koli figures are the settlement, not the fell top. This page carries no warnings. The official page for each place — SMHI in Sweden, FMI in Finland — is linked in the last column and on every card; check it when there is signal. The station records below are the baseline (up to thirty Septembers and Octobers, sample sizes on the table); the forecast is what the next two weeks look like.

Climate recordsSMHI · FMI STATION RECORDS 1996–2025 · ERA5 WIND · NOT A FORECAST

What the last thirty Septembers and Octobers did on these exact dates at the nearest weather station to each base — day and night means, the cold night to plan for, how often the ground was frozen, wet or snow-covered, and the daylight. Use it to pack and to set the rules below; use the SMHI / FMI forecast to decide on the day. Sample sizes vary (Ruka valley 18 years, Sorsele 22, most 28–30); the full tables, sources and method are in Weather_Climate_Normals_v3.1 in the project folder.

Re-route, 9–10 Sep: the trail days now run Ullånger · Granö · Harads, not Strömsund · Sorsele · Jokkmokk; their station rows are below (run 10 Sep), the old three stay for reference, marked off route. The Ullånger and Harads temperature stations sit inland and higher than the hotels (Västmarkum A, 141 m; Lakaträsk A, 185 m), so their frost figures are on the cold side for a village by the water; Granö's station (Vindeln-Sunnansjönäs, 237 m) is a like-for-like inland site.

How to read it. Day / Night are mean daily maximum and minimum. The 1-in-10 night is the 10th-percentile minimum — one night in ten is at least that cold, so expect about three over 29 nights, in runs. Stay met ≤ −5 is the share of past years in which a stay on these exact dates included a night at or below −5 °C. Rain → freeze is ≥ 1 mm on a day above 0 °C followed by a night at or below 0 °C — the black-ice set-up, an upper bound not an ice count. Snow yrs is the share of years with snow on the ground at the station on at least one morning of the stay (Kiruna's series has a 1994–2021 gap; Sorsele has nine years). Gust p90 is the 90th-percentile daily peak gust, ERA5 valley grid — summits are windier. Light is civil dawn to civil dusk on the mid-date.

The fell topsSUMMIT STATIONS · TEMPERATURE ONLY

LeviMuonio Sammaltunturi, 565 m · 35 km from Levi3.4−0.1−4.351 %17 %−6.5 (2009)
SaariselkäKaunispää, ~430 m · above the trailheads3.1−0.7−5.260 %21 %−9.4 (2009)
RukaRukatunturi, ~490 m · the Ruka summit1.6−1.6−5.471 %39 %−11.6 (2002)
Kolino summit station — nearest series is Lieksa Lampela, 27 km away and lower; forecast-only——————
Summits run −1.4 to −2.9 °C colder than their valley by day and about the same at night — what they add is wind and cloud. Rukatunturi stays below zero all day on 39 % of days in the window; Kaunispää on 21 %.

Wind chill and frostbiteENVIRONMENT CANADA INDEX

On a 1-in-10 night with that day's gust, the index from Jukkasjärvi onward runs −8.7 to −13.6 — Environment Canada's moderate band (−10 to −27: hypothermia and frostbite risk if outdoors for long periods without adequate protection). Nothing in thirty years of records reaches the high band (−28 and below, where exposed skin freezes in 10–30 minutes); the nearest is a summit scenario at about −24.2 at Ruka. Frostbite needs air below 0 °C — it is a summit-in-wind problem here; what arrives first everywhere is numb hands, and the wet-cold band every base sits in is where hypothermia actually happens (CDC: possible above 4 °C when wet).

Fell-hike ladder — forecast summit wind chill: ≤ −10 → shorten, add layers, hard turnaround · ≤ −18 → valley walk instead · ≤ −28 → no exposed ground. The −10 and −18 steps are our own; −28 is the cited band.

Rules from the numbersANY ONE TRIGGER → THE SAFER OPTION · PROPOSALS, NOT AUTHORITY

DecisionTrigger — any oneSafer action
Trail day vs the road
every trail option — the alternatives on 28 · 29 Sep and 2 · 4 · 7 · 13 · 17 · 18 Oct, the base-day lines on 1 · 8 · 11 · 15 Oct · decide the evening before, re-check at departure		rain today and tonight's forecast minimum ≤ 0 °C · ≥ 10 mm in 24 h or ≥ 20 mm in 72 h · rain forecast for the trail hours · snow forecast on the route · any SMHI or FMI warning · standing water or deep ruts at the entry · no forecast received and acknowledged before departurean alternative: the day's road plan, on its card · a base-day line: a day at the base, or the card's way off
Fell hike vs valley walkforecast summit wind chill ≤ −10 · forecast summit gusts ≥ 20 m/s · fresh snow ≥ 5 cm · cloud on the summit at departure · a start leaving less than 1.5 × the planned time to the top before the turnaroundsee the ladder above; any other trigger → valley walk
Camping vs lodge vs vehicleforecast minimum below −5 °C · overnight rain with minima near zero · gusts ≥ 15 m/s · any bag without a −10 °C comfort rating (ISO 23537)lodge; if none reachable, sleep in the vehicles with the engine-heat routine and the −10 °C bags, re-plan the next leg
Long transit
17 and 24 Oct		freezing overnight after a wet day · any FMI warning on the route · fog or snow forecastadd 30 % to the drive time; on 24 Oct stay nearer the ferry the night before rather than leave earlier in the dark; most experienced driver on the first, dark hour
Aurora watchforecast minimum ≤ −5 °C or gust ≥ 10 m/sstand-still kit, 30-minute stints, buddy check, return time set — clear aurora nights are the coldest and set up the morning ice
Turnaround on the fells: start descending by sunset − 2 h (Levi 16:29, Saariselkä 16:04, Ruka 15:36, Koli 15:34) and be off the exposed top by sunset − 1 h (17:29, 17:04, 16:36, 16:34). Two headtorches per person regardless. Drivers: the experienced pair take every icy, dark or recovery-prone section; learners drive gravel and easy sections in daylight and dry conditions with an experienced co-driver beside them.

If it goes wrongSOURCED WHERE MARKED · OTHERWISE PRACTICE

  • Wet cold, not deep cold, is the threat. Warning signs (CDC): shivering, exhaustion, confusion, fumbling hands, slurred speech, drowsiness. Action: shelter, wet clothes off, warm the core under dry layers, warm non-alcoholic drink only if fully conscious, handle gently, medical help as soon as possible. With signal, 112; without, the satellite messenger SOS at the same moment — rescue in Lapland is hours away. The messenger needs a clear view of the sky: move to a clearing if a message will not send.
  • Black ice. Wet afternoon, clearing sky, still cold night → glazed road at first light that looks merely damp. Most frequent set-up at Ruka, Saariselkä and Koli. A trail alternative, if taken, starts at first light and the 24 Oct ferry drive starts before it. Speed and following distance are the controls (Liikenneturva).
  • Stuck overnight (NWS): stay in the vehicle; run the engine about 10 minutes each hour, window cracked, exhaust cleared of snow; dome light on at night, bright cloth on the door, bonnet up when the snow stops. Keep above half a tank on remote sections so that routine lasts two nights; a battery CO alarm per vehicle. Convoy: the second vehicle stops short of a soft patch and assesses before following — two vehicles are the recovery plan only until both are stuck.
  • Animals (Liikenneturva): elk move an hour either side of dusk and dawn, September–November — the same hours as the ice and, from mid-October, the same hours you drive. Reindeer everywhere in Lapland, in herds. Slow down on unlit roads, never swerve, 112 after any collision.
  • Trail-day snow: none of the three stations has recorded snow on the ground in the window in its observed years — Ullånger 15, Granö 21, Harads 16 (short series; read as uncommon, not never) — a 06:00 valley reading that says nothing about daytime showers or higher forest roads. Frost on 35–43 % of nights and a wet day on 28–34 % of days. The likely trail hazard is mud, ruts and standing water after rain, then frozen puddles and glazed shade at dawn.

Emergency · Sweden and Finland
112

Police, ambulance, fire, sea and mountain rescue. Free, from any phone on any available network — with or without your own SIM.

It needs a signal. Long stretches of trail between Sundsvall and Hakkas have no mobile coverage. Do not assume you can call for help on the gravel days.

No-signal protocol

  • Satellite messenger or PLB, one per vehicle. On 28–30 September it is the only reliable way to raise anyone.
  • Register for 112 SMS in both countries before departure — a text sometimes gets out where a call will not.
  • Radios and an agreed channel at the start of each gravel leg. Phones are not a convoy tool.
  • Lost contact: the lead stops at the next junction and waits 15 min; the trailing vehicle stops where it is and does not backtrack. If still apart after 15 min, the lead backtracks to the last agreed rendezvous. Radio check every 30 min; never press on hoping to meet.

Trail alternatives · the rules28 SEP – 18 OCT · IF A TRAIL DAY IS TAKEN

  • Sweden, 28 Sep – 2 Oct: paved is the plan, gravel is the option (Adam, 17 Sep). The four cards carry the E4 · E12 · 97 · E10 plans — 116 · 191 · 351 · 279 km, 1.9 · 3.2 · 5.5 · 4.8 h, leaving 12:00 · 10:00 · 09:30 · 10:00 — and the S-07, S-07 + S-06 and S-16 lines as alternatives with their own bars, timelines and cut rules; the S-06 line is not an alternative but the 1 Oct gravel day from Treehotel, driven from the gate to Arvidsjaur, gravel first, a base day with no bed to reach (Adam, 24 Sep) — 881 km of trail line in all, ~214 km of it reads tarmac in NVDB, ~662 gravel, ~5 unrecorded. The choice is made at breakfast; the beds are booked either way.
  • A Swedish trail alternative departs at first light — 06:30; the Finnish ones at 07:00–07:15, as on their cards. At 35 km/h on gravel the long second day, the 29th, needs every minute of it: a check-in window that closes at 21:00, no mobile coverage for most of the way, and a surface nobody has confirmed — the early start is the whole margin for a puncture, a washout, or driving on when a bed falls through. Tell the hotel which plan you are on when you give the arrival time.
  • Distances are measured from the GPX; hours are 35 km/h on recorded gravel and 65 on recorded tarmac (an hour of stops, 06:30 start) — Adam's planning figures. The GPX carries no surface data, so the surface comes from Trafikverket's NVDB and Finland's Digiroad, sampled about every kilometre along every line on 22–24 Sep (analysis/Surface_Verification_v1.2.md) — up to ~6 km apart where the GPX is drawn coarsely, so each sample counts for the stretch around it (v1.30); unrecorded stretches are timed at the gravel speed but not counted as gravel. S-07 on the 28th is about half tarmac; S-16 is four-fifths gravel, S-06 nine-tenths. The Swedish alternatives come out at ~6.8, ~9.3 and ~7.6 h; the 1 Oct line at ~10.3 h from 06:30 with an hour of stops (Storforsen ~30 min inside it — the only visit), gravel first; the cut at Moskosel is on the card. The 28th is the calibration — if the real average is under 35, take the road on the 29th.
  • All three trail beds are booked (10 Sep; the 28th moved to Docksta on the 16th) — Docksta Hotell on the 28th and Granö Beckasin on the 29th, both free to cancel until 26–27 Sep and both wanting an arrival time; Treehotel on the 30th, non-refundable, SEK 16,600, check-in until 22:00. If a trail day is ending short of its bed, ring the hotel from the last town with signal and finish on the road — a no-show costs the night, and the road reaches all three. The Treehotel night and the Icehotel arrival are the two worth protecting.
  • Finland, 4–18 Oct: the same rule (Adam, 10 Sep). Five alternatives sit on the cards with their own bars, timelines and cut rules — A′ · B · D · H7 · K1, 840 km of trail line, of which ~327 km reads tarmac and ~192 km gravel in Digiroad and ~321 km has no record — and the choice is made at departure. Three lines are not alternatives but base-day gravel days with no bed to reach (Adam, 24 Sep): FIN-07 north of Inari on Thu 8 Oct (123 km), the FIN-07 line from Glass Resort on 11 Oct (191 km) and the FIN-01 border line from the Ruka lodge on Thu 15 Oct (197 km). K1 on the 18th is now the long day (~9.5 h); on K1 the margin falls on tarmac, not on the trail. First light is 07:00–07:15 by then and last light 18:20–19:00.
  • Lentiira, 17 Oct — booked for two adults in one chalet: extra beds or a second chalet for four, linen and towels ×4 (or pack them), and dinner, all to be arranged with the property in advance; the kitchen there is limited. Koli's new confirmation (18–21 Oct) shows one adult per room — re-correct to four.
  • Decide by mid-afternoon, not at dusk. Last light ~19:00 and falling a few minutes a day. Arriving in the dark near freezing, on gravel, is a different job from arriving at 17:00.
  • Ring ahead from the last town with signal. Granö's check-in window ends at 21:00 and Treehotel's at 22:00, and both confirmations ask for an expected arrival time; Granö's restaurant opens on request only, so dinner for four is ordered on the same call. Numbers on each leg and in the lodging manifest.
  • Expect frost. Station records for the three bases (1996–2025): Ullånger 28–29 Sep night 2.4 °C, frost 35 % of nights, one night in ten −2.9; Granö 29–30 Sep night 3.0, frost 22 %, one in ten −1.5; Harads 30 Sep–1 Oct night 0.9, frost 43 %, one in ten −4.3 — coldest night in the Harads window −9.8 (2009). Plan for −5 °C, not 0.
  • Check the sleeping bags before Arlanda. A +5 °C bag is not a −5 °C bag, and the difference arrives at 03:00.
  • Allemansrätten permits a night or two on undeveloped land, away from homes and in nobody's way. It does not permit driving off-road to reach a spot.

WildlifeTHE MAIN ROAD HAZARD IN LAPLAND

  • Moose and reindeer, at first and last light — exactly when the trail days start. Slow down on unlit sections; a moose is dark, tall and does not reflect headlights.
  • Never swerve. Brake hard in a straight line. A swerve into a ditch or a tree is the worse outcome.
  • A collision with moose, reindeer or deer must be reported — call 112 in both countries and mark the spot. Reindeer are somebody's property.

ConditionsSTATION RECORDS · SEE WEATHER TAB

  • Trail bases (Ullånger/Docksta, Granö, Harads): run 10 Sep. Ullånger 28–29 Sep: day 11.1 / night 2.4 °C, frost 35 % (inland station, 141 m — the village will run milder); Granö 29–30 Sep: day 9.9 / night 3.0, frost 22 %; Harads 30 Sep–1 Oct: day 8.4 / night 0.9, frost 43 %, rain-then-freeze set-up on 11 % of days. No snow on the ground recorded in any of the three windows (15–21 years of record).
  • Jukkasjärvi, 1–4 Oct: day 6.0 / night 0.2 °C; frost 48 % of nights; the stay met a frost in 76 % of past years and a night ≤ −5 °C in 24 %; snow on the ground in 50 % of years (record 33 cm, 1966).
  • No polar night. The first sunless day at Kiruna's latitude is 11 December.

Pre-gravel checks

  • Tyres — settled. Both vehicles on winter tyres. Not legally required until 1 Nov (FI) and 1 Dec (SE), so this is ahead of the statute rather than because of it.
  • Fuel. Top up at every station on the gravel days — the fuel towns are named on each trail card, from the TET file. Rural pumps are often unmanned, card-only automats: carry two different chip-and-PIN cards. Tank range on gravel and whether jerrycans are carried: confirm with Ronny.
  • Recovery. Rated recovery points, strap, shackles, traction boards, shovel, compressor — verified at Arlanda, and someone who knows how to use them.
  • Tyres, second line. Plug kit and valve tool per vehicle; confirm each has a full-size spare. A vehicle without a serviceable spare drops to the sealed route. Tyre service towns on or near the line: Härnösand, Umeå, Arvidsjaur, Gällivare — not checked.
  • Satellite messengers — confirmed? One per vehicle, registered, tested before Sundsvall. Without them the trail days are a no-go.
  • Cold-camp minimum per vehicle — sleep system rated below zero, two days' food and water, headtorches, first-aid kit. Checked at Arlanda alongside the tent.
  • If a vehicle stops: get off the carriageway, hazards on, warm layers on, then call — 112 for injury, Ronny for the vehicle. Share position as decimal coordinates from the map app.

What gives way to what

When two things conflict: safety and road conditions first · the ferry and the flights second · the three non-refundable arrivals third · the trail fourth · everything under Things to do last. Every rule on this page follows from that order.

Open5 ITEMS

  • Which TET edition is on Ronny's navigation unit. The 2022 main line (S-04 → S-05: Bräcke · Backe · Åsele · Lycksele · Arvidsjaur · Moskosel · Kåbdalis · Jokkmokk · Hakkas) never touches Strömsund or Sorsele; the roadbook now follows S-07 → S-06 → S-16 of the 2026 file. Ask when he makes contact which edition and which sections his unit carries, and load the 2026 file on your own device regardless.
  • Northern trail passability, late September. Unverified. Contingency route under Distance.

The ferry docks 06:10 and the vehicles go back first; at Arlanda by ~15:00. Alternative on the card: the 12:40 → 14:55 (US$1,810) — only if Ronny confirms an early Sunday handover.
  • Handover time on 27 Sep, and where the vehicles go back on 25 Oct — both Sundays. the pickup point is now the Stockholm hotel, Ett Hem (Adam, 21 Sep) — Ronny still needs the address and the handover arrangement is unconfirmed; the return point is not yet agreed (the 43 km Värtahamnen → Arlanda run is in the leg table). Ronny makes contact about a week out; ask before then, since the whole first day is timed off an assumed 08:00 handover.
